## Diagnostic D71: do causally known asset regimes explain distinct strategy behavior?

**Registered before reading new per-asset outcome groups:** The user notes that Ronnie appears to discuss a relatively small set of mainstream coins while the fixed backtest spans 37 perpetuals. Source cases are a curated, incomplete set, not a census of his traded assets; video mentions do not prove an exchange order. Keep the full registered 37-coin shared-account runs and the fixed 2026-coin backcast limitation. Test whether *predecision* asset properties (volatility, wickiness, trend versus range) are useful descriptors of H15a/H19a and the separately registered H22a, without coin-specific strategies or selecting profitable tickers. At each UTC month start, use only the preceding **60 complete native daily LAST bars** per coin from the existing daily Catalog: annualized close-to-close realized volatility `std(log(C_t/C_(t-1))) * sqrt(365)`, median daily wick fraction `(high-max(open,close)+min(open,close)-low)/(high-low)` on positive-range days, and trend efficiency `abs(C_last-C_first)/sum(abs(C_t-C_(t-1)))`. Missing/nonpositive prices or insufficient bars are an unavailable feature state, never filled from future months. For each feature independently, rank the 37 coins into low/middle/high **cross-sectional terciles** at that month start using a deterministic symbol tie-break; do not search thresholds, multiply factors into 27 tiny cells, or fit a prediction model on this year. Map each native Position to its *first entry-order submission month* and inherited feature state; use native realized net PnL including fees/funding and the original actual entered stop-risk denominator only for positions closed by report end, and preserve open/right-censored positions separately. Report the exposure and win/net-R distribution by each prespecified tercile and each strategy, plus count by coin and month, as descriptive development evidence; group PnL is not an independently funded account return. Compare source-case mentioned assets against the 37-pool inventory as provenance, without treating curated source frequency or profitable group labels as a trade eligibility filter. With many correlated group contrasts and a viewed annual set, no asset class is validated by a positive point estimate; any proposed asset-policy child needs a separate causal mechanism, a frozen rule and full Nautilus paired shared-account replay with multiplicity-aware delayed/forward qualification. D71 should follow H22a's full-pool result so it cannot select whether H22a runs.

**Input coverage correction before any D71 outcome grouping:** A native Catalog probe found that the standalone downloaded daily Catalog ends on **2026-08-31**, while the registered five-minute LAST/MARK replay continues to 2026-10-07. D71's monthly features must therefore use daily warmup Catalog bars only before the common replay start, then aggregate each complete UTC day from the **same registered five-minute LAST Catalog** used by the runner; at month starts use only days strictly before 00:00 UTC. Verify 288 contiguous five-minute bars and exact UTC daily close for each aggregate, and hash the input Catalog trees. This preserves the originally registered 60-day features and causal timing; it does not fill late months with unavailable daily-download data or revise feature definitions after looking at outcomes.

**Read-only outcome and scope:** Frozen method registration `1eaebfbcd` and input-coverage correction `d44cdd3df` preceded [the D71 native asset-regime read](results/2026-10-08-d71-asset-regimes.json), SHA-256 `0d1b12ca483aa95f1ec4c0ed944223a5f43362c59dc28db1fc9caa53d4b080be`; [all native Position mappings](results/2026-10-08-d71-asset-regime-positions.json.gz) have SHA-256 `624d4dd60a6742e6811c7e66733e971a1faeaeb803814e6af61034e8e9d7b5e9`. The reader verifies all 37 registered minute and daily Catalog tree hashes, 288 contiguous five-minute LAST bars per complete day, the native order/Position/account report hashes and three Strategy audits before grouping. There are **481/481 available coin-month feature states** across 13 UTC month starts and 37 coins; no missing features were imputed. H15a was replayed with the current published-Nautilus runner and passes its [native OTO audit](results/2026-10-08-d71-h15a-native-audit.json), SHA-256 `4a3393a64d2ba24d79c487c4b64ad5e22426d2069a9e03027496bf2b053c4e37`. Its raw order/fill/Position counts and final equity exactly match the older H15a control: **29,712 / 2,738 / 686 / 104,160.59375166 USDT**. The existing strict `compare.py` [receipt](results/2026-10-08-d71-h15a-parity.json) reports `parity_passed=false` solely because the current runner added a per-coin `entry_line_cancel: null` report key; all native order/fill differences are empty and equity/commission gaps are zero. Do not call this full JSON parity; the new same-code H15a run is the D71 source of truth. All three H15a/H19a/H22a groups use native Position first-order **submission month**, native closed net PnL including commissions/funding and actual original-stop entered risk; open Positions remain right-censored.

The prespecified **realized-volatility** terciles in H19a have low/middle/high closed samples **171/161/164**, wins **47.4%/44.1%/36.6%** and mean net R **+0.435/+0.284/+0.166**. The prespecified daily **wick-fraction** terciles in H15a have **242/230/204** closes, wins **59.1%/58.7%/51.0%**, mean net R **+0.190/+0.201/+0.064**; H22a has **230/220/212**, wins **54.8%/55.0%/51.4%**, mean net R **+0.147/+0.143/+0.067**. Trend-efficiency tertiles show no consistent monotone win-rate separation across these three strategies; all feature/run/tercile counts, native net-PnL sums, censored counts, per-coin and per-month counts are in the artifact, including groups that look unfavorable. These are **post-exposure descriptive associations**, not independent predictive validation or group-funded account returns. D71 supports an asset-state *mechanism question* and the product need to record causal feature availability, cohort size and right-censoring, but authorizes no winning-coin list, per-coin preset, volatility/"wick" filter or improved annual return claim. A next child should distinguish persistent asset traits from changing market regimes and actual five-minute spike/Mark divergence before freezing any single selection or sizing rule; account risk and delayed/forward qualification remain mandatory.

This does not promote H22a or prove a new economic edge.

## Diagnostic D72: were native protective exits associated with five-minute LAST spikes absent in MARK?

**Registered before reading D72 outcomes:** Use the already frozen, audited full-37 native H19a and H15a Position/fill/order reports, and the registered five-minute LAST and MARK Catalogs; make no new Strategy, order or PnL simulation. Match every native long Position with a completed STOP_MARKET closing fill to that fill's exact five-minute event bar and its native parent-linked stop trigger. Verify input Catalog tree digests against the registered identity, 105,120 contiguous LAST and MARK bars per coin, report hashes against their independent native audits, and that the stop fill maps to exactly one bar. The strict *LAST-only stop-bar crossing* indicator is `LAST low <= native stop trigger < MARK low` on that same bar, with both prior completed five-minute LAST and MARK closes above the trigger. Also report the broader same-bar crossing without the prior-close requirement, total native stop exits, and distribution of `(MARK low - LAST low) / prior LAST close` in basis points at stop bars. This comparison tests whether trade-price spikes can mechanically affect native stop exits; it is not a counterfactual MARK-triggered order result because OHLC lacks intrabar order, spread and execution sequence. Count missing/misaligned event bars as failures, not as zero divergence.

**Asset/regime connection and decision boundary:** For each stopped Position, inherit its D71 first-order-submission-month daily-wick tercile, preserving all three terciles and censored positions in the denominator report. Report LAST-only counts/fractions and native net-PnL by tercile for H19a and H15a, plus coin and month counts, without picking a favorable coin or threshold. The D71 daily-wick feature is an imperfect earlier proxy for a subsequent five-minute spike; this read is descriptive and on the exposed year. If LAST-only stop crossings are rare, the next child should focus on structure and trend context rather than mark-trigger substitution. If they are material and cluster by a causal prior feature, investigate exchange trigger semantics and possible late-period or forward validation before preregistering one common protective-order rule. No observed D72 bucket authorizes changing the 37-coin pool, per-coin settings, capital allocation, live orders or the Goal score.

**Unit clarification before any D72 bar/outcome read:** The primary unit is a native Position with at least one STOP_MARKET fill; match its **first chronological** stop fill to the bar and stop trigger, then count later stop fills separately. A multi-tier Position can have several stop fills, and a mixed stop/target Position is still called *stop-associated*, never assumed wholly closed by a stop. The denominator is all closed native Positions in each inherited D71 class, with open Positions reported separately as right-censored. Do not silently discard a mixed exit or treat each tier as an independent trade.

**Input-end correction before outcome read:** D71 needed 105,120 bars per series for 365 *complete UTC daily groups*, ending at 2026-10-07 00:00 UTC. The native annual replay and possible exit fills continue another 8.5 hours to 08:30 UTC. D72 must instead require **105,222** contiguous five-minute event bars for both LAST and MARK per coin through that exact end time, with no late exits dropped. This corrects D72's coverage count only; its crossing definition and native Position unit remain fixed.

**Native read and mechanism finding:** Preregistration `613d54558`, Position-unit clarification `170ca0a27` and input-end correction `760268c53` preceded the read-only implementation `df7bfd6fd`. The [37-coin D72 summary](results/2026-10-08-d72-native-stop-spikes.json), SHA-256 `d619236776eec866757a63f439bca8ca30f043d5e3a78fb97aa93db8a5390417`, and [every mapped native Position](results/2026-10-08-d72-native-stop-spike-positions.json), SHA-256 `a27b83df95f9e30034cffc952257f9803b25ecc95f7be8085afa3b56e99f3ae1`, bind the D71 Position identity, both independent native report audits, all 37 registered Catalog tree digests and **105,222** contiguous LAST plus **105,222** MARK bars per coin. Each first native protective stop fill matched its exact completed five-minute event bar and predecessor; no missing/misaligned bars were converted into non-crossings. H19a has **496 closed/11 open** native Positions, **266** stop-associated Positions (**516** stop-fill events), and **78/266 = 29.32%** first-stop bars with `LAST low <= trigger < MARK low` and both prior closes above the trigger. H15a has **676 closed/10 open**, **175** stop-associated Positions (**502** stop-fill events), and **46/175 = 26.29%** strict LAST-only first-stop bars. All **266/175** first-stop bars crossed the stop in LAST; MARK crossed in **188/129**. Strict and broader same-bar LAST-only counts coincide in these runs. Median `(MARK low - LAST low)/prior LAST close` at first-stop bars is **14.63 bp** H19a and **10.67 bp** H15a; the respective 90th percentiles are **68.81/73.00 bp**. Those are native stop-bar observations, not a synthetic MARK-stop trade result.

**Asset connection and next action:** The preregistered *earlier* daily-wick tertiles do not concentrate LAST-only stop bars in the predicted high-wick class: H19a low/middle/high have **33/90, 19/85, 26/91** strict crossings per stop-associated Position; H15a **19/59, 16/58, 11/58**. All closed, open, stopped, coin and submission-month counts remain in the summary; stop-associated net PnL includes native fees/funding and can include target fills in the same Position, so it is not an avoidable-loss estimate. This rejects the simple daily-wick proxy as a validated stop-spike selector and is a second-level result, not abandonment of the asset-state mechanism. The **actual five-minute LAST/MARK divergence** deserves a separate causal predecision feature study, along with confirmation of exchange/native protective trigger semantics and the cost of later or missed exits. Do not retune a wick cutoff or switch stops to MARK from this exposed annual read; any protective or asset rule still needs one frozen common specification, full paired native shared-account replay and delayed/forward qualification.

## Diagnostic D73: does prior five-minute downside LAST/MARK divergence persist into native stop events?

**Registered before new D73 feature/outcome grouping:** D72 found substantial same-bar LAST-only first-stop crossings in H19a/H15a, while D71's prior daily-wick tercile failed as a proxy. Keep both native strategies, the full 37-coin shared-account reports, all native Positions and the fixed five-minute LAST/MARK Catalogs; do not alter orders or estimate hypothetical fills. At each UTC month start **2026-01-01 through 2026-10-01**, compute for every coin the **99th percentile** of `max(0, MARK low - LAST low) / previous completed five-minute LAST close * 10,000` over exactly the previous **60 complete UTC days** (17,280 paired five-minute bars). The 99th percentile is a frozen tail descriptor, not a tuned trade cutoff. Verify positive prices, contiguous synchronized pairs and each registered Catalog tree digest. October–December 2025 order-submission months are **unavailable** because the reused five-minute Catalog begins October 7; retain those Positions separately and never use future bars or the daily-only warmup to invent MARK features.

**Prespecified read and falsifier:** Rank all 37 available coin features separately at each month start into low/middle/high cross-sectional terciles with symbol tie-break, using no trade outcomes. Join each native Position to its first entry-order submission month, D71 original native net R/PnL and D72 first-stop-bar classification. For each variant and each tercile, report closed/open Positions, stop-associated Positions, strict LAST-only count **per stopped and per all closed**, native win/net-R/PnL, coin/month counts and the unavailable group. Also report how often a coin changes tercile across adjacent months and, for coins with both low and high stopped exposures, the direction/ties of its high-minus-low strict LAST-only rate; do not pool independent-account returns or claim a significance test from sparse observations. A useful predecision proxy would at minimum show a coherent high-versus-low separation in both H19a and H15a and nontrivial within-coin support. If it fails, do not sweep the quantile or lookback on this exposed year; investigate stop trigger semantics, market state and source entry validity before proposing another rule. Even a descriptive success cannot select assets or qualify the Goal without a separately preregistered, full shared-account native paired Strategy and delayed/forward verification.

**Read-only result and falsification:** Registration `a23b6e85d` preceded implementation/result commit `0a6f89a10`. The [D73 feature and outcome summary](results/2026-10-08-d73-prior-gap-states.json), SHA-256 `e5879bd6362e9de1fc5be941b149611de62427bb68960602b5b717e862b31780`, and [all mapped Positions](results/2026-10-08-d73-prior-gap-positions.json), SHA-256 `75d5dfd29fc219239fdee27b6898e1348bb0eea5cdbfd67fe7cc1fa6f43db4c`, verify all 37 registered Catalog tree hashes, each **17,280** paired prior bars and **370/370** causal coin-month states for January–October 2026. Every month has 13/12/12 low/middle/high ranked coins. The earlier October–December 2025 submission months remain unavailable: **73** H19a and **80** H15a closed Positions, none filled from future MARK data. In **68/333** adjacent coin-month transitions the tercile changes; **10** coins never switch, and only **four** experience both low and high across the ten starts. Thus this descriptor is partly a persistent asset property, and its across-coin groups do not by themselves isolate a changing within-asset regime.

H19a low/middle/high groups have **140/132/151** closed Positions, native win **50.7%/43.9%/38.4%** and mean realized net R **+0.491/+0.362/+0.180**. H15a has **221/194/181** closes, wins **55.7%/57.7%/56.9%** and mean net R **+0.173/+0.148/+0.123**. These exposed-year PnL associations, especially H19a's low/high difference, cannot be read as a funded portfolio-filter return or a coin-specific edge. Among stopped Positions, strict first-stop LAST-only crossings for H19a low/middle/high are **18/69 (26.1%), 27/74 (36.5%), 27/90 (30.0%)**; H15a **9/53 (17.0%), 18/46 (39.1%), 12/50 (24.0%)**. High exceeds low in both, but neither profile is monotone and the middle class has the highest incidence. Only **two** H19a coins and **one** H15a coin have both low- and high-class stopped exposure; their sparse directions cannot support a within-coin mechanism. The preregistered joint proxy gate therefore fails. No quantile/lookback sweep, asset exclusion, MARK-stop counterfactual or strategy promotion follows. Preserve this as a second-level negative asset-feature finding; next investigate actual Binance/Nautilus stop trigger semantics and source-defined entry/market context before freezing a new protective or participation rule.

## Diagnostic D74: can pinned Nautilus replay a Binance-style MARK protective trigger with the present data stream?

**Source/API read before native probe:** [Binance's current USD-M conditional-order documentation](https://developers.binance.com/en/docs/catalog/core-trading-derivatives-trading-usd-s-m-futures/api/rest-api/trade#new-algo-order-trade) permits `workingType=MARK_PRICE` or `CONTRACT_PRICE` for a conditional `STOP_MARKET`; the latter is its documented default, while `priceProtect` is a separate optional condition. [Nautilus' order model](https://nautilustrader.io/docs/latest/concepts/orders/) names both `DEFAULT` and `MARK_PRICE` trigger types, and the locally pinned `nautilus_trader==2.0.0rc3` `OrderFactory.bracket` signature accepts `sl_trigger_type`. These docs do not prove the current Binance live adapter maps the choice correctly, nor that this particular backtest feed processes it. Inspection of the current registered `strategies/r1/run_portfolio.py` finds that it converts each five-minute MARK Catalog bar to exactly **one `MarkPriceUpdate` at that bar's close/time**, then feeds LAST OHLC bars. Thus D72/D73's MARK *low* comparisons are valid descriptive archived-bar readings but are **not** native MARK-stop trigger replays under the current close-only MARK stream. Do not treat the 78/46 LAST-only low crossings as avoidable exits or a MARK-trigger PnL estimate.

**Frozen native capability probe before economic change:** Using one registered BTC native Instrument, the same pinned published Nautilus `BacktestEngine`, 100,000-USDT margin account, native bracket with one entry, one target and a stop, compare only `sl_trigger_type=DEFAULT` versus `MARK_PRICE` on an identical synthetic sequence. First fill the native entry. Then send a five-minute LAST bar whose low crosses the stop while its MARK close remains above; require DEFAULT to stop while MARK_PRICE remains protected/open. On a later event send a MARK close below the stop while LAST does not newly cross; require MARK_PRICE to trigger, and capture native order statuses, timestamps, fill prices, OCO cancellation, denials/rejections and account state. If these exact separations cannot be proven, record the limitation rather than synthesizing a parallel trigger engine. Even if they pass, the annual MARK-close stream is coarser than an exchange-evaluated mark trigger: any subsequent Strategy candidate requires explicit data-frequency/slippage limitations, one frozen common rule, full 37-coin native paired replay, and risk checks for delayed or worse exit. No live order, API key or credentials are involved.

**Failed native attempts and registered isolating child:** First fixture [attempt 1](results/2026-10-08-d74-mark-trigger-native-probe-attempt1.json), SHA-256 `7219d755dc8430c665189e2cb12acd20fc970464914e197e456a9548257f1e88`, submitted `Quantity.from_str("0.01")` against a BTC Instrument requiring three decimal places; Nautilus rejected entry and contingent orders. This was a fixture precision error, not a trigger result. After using `Instrument.make_qty(0.01)`, [attempt 2](results/2026-10-08-d74-mark-trigger-native-probe-attempt2.json), SHA-256 `31718715a3d9ccfee7cf782e65bbc5c7ea071f2707864a0720dda858b44b961c`, has filled native entries, no rejection and both `DEFAULT` **and** `MARK_PRICE` stops filled on the same LAST-low-crossing bar, despite the fed MARK close staying above stop. The exact D74 separation gate fails; `trigger_type` is preserved in the order report but that alone does not prove matcher semantics. Before any new economic replay, run one fixed isolating path with the same native bracket/account/instrument: keep **every post-entry LAST bar low above stop** while one later `MarkPriceUpdate` close falls below stop. If only MARK_PRICE then fires, bar execution appears to ignore the requested type on LAST crossings; if neither fires, MARK updates may not drive conditional matching here; if DEFAULT fires too, default/mark routing is conflated. Preserve both outcomes and stop this route if the capability remains unproven; do not relabel a Python OHLC comparison as native MARK execution.

**Isolating child result and decision:** [The two-path pinned native probe](results/2026-10-08-d74-mark-trigger-native-probe.json), SHA-256 `8176b7ed3daef974c9a0d1374fe653176ef490e90fbd0ea287f08b9ed335bc73`, source commit `0ce02c576`, reproduces attempt 2 on the first path. In the preregistered **MARK-close-only crossing** path, both bracket entries fill at 99,000 USDT, all subsequent LAST bar lows stay at 97,000 above the 95,000 stop, and MARK close falls to 94,000. The `DEFAULT` and `MARK_PRICE` stop orders both remain `ACCEPTED`, targets remain `ACCEPTED`, and both native Positions stay open/protected. Across both paths there are no new denied/rejected orders. Thus this exact published-rc3 `bar_execution=True` + LAST OHLC + once-per-bar `MarkPriceUpdate` setup **does not differentiate MARK from DEFAULT stops**: a LAST low triggers both; a MARK close alone triggers neither. It would be false to change only `sl_trigger_type` and call a new annual run a MARK-stop backtest. The current [Nautilus OrderEmulator documentation](https://nautilustrader.io/docs/latest/concepts/orders/emulated/) says its local `emulation_trigger` supports quote/last-trade choices, not MARK_PRICE; the local version would require its own separate proof. Do not build a Python stop matcher or select MARK from the exposed D72 counts. Retain the valid DEFAULT/LAST H19a replay; treat genuine MARK-trigger capability as an upstream/native data-and-matching investigation separate from the Ronnie entry/sizing research. The adjacent native funding Catalog opportunity should likewise be accepted only after its own full-37 paired cash/equity parity, not inferred from this stop probe.

## Diagnostic D75: do asset-state differences survive comparison within the same coin and calendar month?

**Registration before the new contrast read:** D71 already defined, before grouping outcomes, three causal 60-complete-day monthly features and cross-sectional low/middle/high ranks for every one of the fixed 37 Binance perpetuals. Its H19a low-volatility group had better exposed-year native results than its high-volatility group, while D72/D73 rejected simple wick and LAST/MARK-gap explanations for stop behavior. Reuse the exact D71 feature-state artifact and all mapped **native** H15a/H19a/H22a Positions; verify both frozen SHA-256 identities, 481 unique coin-month states, 37 symbols, 13 ordered month starts, each Position's unique ID and inherited feature ranks, and closed/open totals against the D71 report. No hypothetical fills or independent coin accounts enter this diagnostic.

**Frozen comparisons and decision:** For *each* of the three existing features and *each* of the three strategies, report all low/middle/high closed and open Position counts, native closed net-R/win/PnL, and the number of adjacent monthly rank changes out of 444 possible coin transitions. Then compare **high minus low** on closed Positions by (a) coin: only coins observed with at least one closed Position in both ranks, each coin receiving equal weight, and (b) UTC entry-submission month: only months with both ranks, each month receiving equal weight. Preserve each coin/month contrast, denominator and positive/negative/tied directions, rather than hiding sparse cases behind a pooled average. A Position is assigned to its first native entry-order submission month; open Positions remain censored, never counted as losses or wins. In both comparisons use native realized net R with fees/funding and the native closed win indicator; group PnL is not a separately funded return. If H19a's apparent low-volatility advantage disappears or reverses within coins or within months, reject low-volatility asset selection as an evidenced next rule; if both contrasts align, treat it only as a source-led candidate for **one** future common 37-coin native shared-account rule, subject to capital effects and delayed/forward/multiplicity-aware validation. Check wick and trend in full as negative/secondary context, with no choice of whichever looks best. These viewed-year descriptive contrasts cannot qualify the Goal or prove Ronnie traded only the eight coins mentioned in our curated video set.

**Read-only result and decision:** Registration `7751afd3d` preceded the [D75 frozen-input contrast artifact](results/2026-10-08-d75-within-asset-regimes.json), SHA-256 `86d2bba85486a51a6f568e9a1724c6636fecf1d0a1d0d60003b968ded832c0d5`. Its reader verifies D71's exact summary and Position SHA-256, all 481 unique 37-coin/13-month feature states, all 1,883 unique native Positions, inherited ranks, closed/open totals and every original feature/variant/rank count, win and native PnL sum. Prior-volatility ranks change in **144/444** adjacent coin-month transitions; **17/37** coins ever occupy both low and high ranks. Prior wick ranks change **220/444**, trend-efficiency ranks **275/444**. For H19a volatility, only **11** coins actually have closed Positions in both low and high ranks: equal-coin high-minus-low mean net R is **−0.672**, with **9/11** negative coin contrasts, and equal-month high-minus-low is **−0.352**, with **8/11** negative monthly contrasts. Thus its original low-volatility association does not disappear under these two separate comparisons. However, several comparable coins have only one or two closed Positions in a rank, neither comparison controls coin and month simultaneously, and Position inclusion conditions on candidate formation, submission, fill and closure. H15a volatility gives **−0.340 R** within coin but **+0.087 R** within month; H22a gives **−0.340/+0.051 R**. A common low-volatility gate is not supported across the three variants. H15a's wick high-minus-low is **−0.347 R** across 28 comparable coins (23 negative) and **−0.133 R** across 11 comparable months (7 negative), while H19a is near zero/mixed (**−0.056/+0.046 R**) and H22a is **−0.103/−0.079 R**; trend efficiency has no coherent cross-variant direction. This remains viewed-year mechanism evidence, **not** an independently verified asset-class alpha or a funded filter result. Next assess how volatility relates to H19a source-aligned *candidate and fill selection*, plus simultaneous account risk; only then could one source-motivated, cross-coin rule be frozen for 37-coin native paired and delayed/forward qualification. Do not select the 11 comparable coins, optimize tercile edges, or assign per-coin strategies from this artifact.

## Diagnostic D77: does H19a's source-aligned planned and filled order geometry differ by prior volatility state?

**Registration before geometry-group read:** The C20/C21 BTC source distinguishes 50%/61.8% pullback entries, a shared outside invalidation and room to a prior high; C22 warns that a small break inside a wider range can fail. Match D76's bundle identity, submission month, rank and first-positive-fill status; preserve the seven multi-Position bundles separately. No orders, fills or returns are simulated.

**Frozen read and decision:** At *submission*, compute each tier's planned `(target-entry)/(entry-stop)` in initial R, `(entry-stop)/entry` stop distance and `(target-entry)/entry` target room, using native order prices. Report low/middle/high count and median/10th/90th percentiles for **all** submitted bundles and for the pre-existing native positively filled subset, separately for first and deeper tier, plus each month's and coin's bundle counts. For each positively filled bundle, use its **first chronological native BUY fill** price and that parent's original stop/target to compute actual fill-to-target initial R and fill-to-stop distance; record which tier filled first and any invalid/ambiguous geometry rather than silently dropping it. Do not use ex-post PnL or stop outcome to choose a geometry cutoff. If high-volatility submitted or filled bundles already have materially worse target-to-invalidation geometry at both tiers, source-aligned room may help explain D75; if geometry is similar or better, prioritize the first 1/3/5/10 complete four-hour post-fill bars, MFE/MAE and structural persistence against native exits. Any new participation or sizing rule needs its own frozen source/mechanism specification, full 37-coin Nautilus shared-account pairing, unchanged 100,000-USDT denominator and delayed/forward multiplicity-aware validation; D77 alone does not authorize a filter or Goal score.

**Read-only result and mechanism decision:** Preregistration `52e92ab5f` preceded the [D77 all-bundle summary](results/2026-10-08-d77-native-entry-geometry.json), SHA-256 `0c358599d9956634c292f0a7efda2a4049088f25b2ad690fce283e22537c44e6`, and [per-bundle native order geometry](results/2026-10-08-d77-native-entry-geometry-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `ed66b59b9f8db091572aaec1fb65146c629686cc94954fa3cb6fc991034c56b0`. All 2,939 submitted bundles and 5,878 native parent/stop/target bracket triplets have unique matched native IDs and valid shared `stop < deep entry < first entry < B target` geometry; 500 bundles have native BUY fills and exactly match D76's prior class/identity. At submission, low/middle/high prior-volatility groups have first-tier median planned target-to-stop R **1.738/1.723/1.717** and deeper-tier **3.641/3.589/3.568**. Among the *actually filled* bundles, first-tier medians are **1.734/1.724/1.722**; for the uniquely identifiable first native BUY fill, target-to-original-stop medians are **1.733/1.724/1.721 R**. Thus high-volatility H19a does **not** carry a large systematic ex-ante R/R deficit that could by itself explain its 10.8-point lower closed win rate. Its first-tier planned stop distance is much wider in percentage terms—median **8.12%** of entry versus **4.95%** in low volatility—while proportional target room also rises **14.08% versus 8.52%**, leaving normalized R/R similar. Same-timestamp earliest BUY fills prevent a unique first-tier attribution in **3/174 low**, **6/166 middle** and **14/160 high** positively filled bundles; all are retained, not chosen by CSV row order or omitted from the denominator. The target in this diagnostic is H19a's researcher-defined broad B and not proven to be Ronnie's nearest obstacle in a specific source case. The next read-only mechanism child should observe 1/3/5/10 fully completed four-hour bars **after** the first native fill, price MFE/MAE in its original risk units, approximate origin/support persistence, native stop/target/time censoring, later tier fills and eventual net outcomes. Test slow-winner counterexamples before freezing any time/structure exit. No annual score or asset participation rule changes here.

## Diagnostic D79: can asset-class or first-bar-stop repair alone close the joint Goal gap?

**Registration before new capacity arithmetic:** D78 found only 24 native Positions stopped before the first complete four-hour landmark, with 16 in the high-volatility rank and several duplicated across two-Position bundles. Before building a shock-day exclusion, per-coin rule or blanket stop modification, bind the exact D78 summary/detail hashes above and H19a native `summary.json` SHA-256 `cd0212f4c6880469c5d5b9db0b8b931618590fc2914bff42417e95ea9e41b555`. Read native realized net PnL including fees/funding only for the fixed first-landmark stopped set and for the high-rank closed group; preserve native Position and distinct bundle counts separately. This is a **nontradable fixed-path arithmetic limit**, never a new Strategy, order, fill or shared-account return.

**Frozen bounds and decision:** Calculate the exact annualized >20% final-equity threshold from the registered 100,000-USDT initial account and 2025-10-17 00:00 to 2026-10-07 08:30 UTC eligible duration; compare it with the H19a final equity. Report (a) removing only the 24 early stopped losses from both closed-trade denominator and realized PnL while holding all other native trades/equity fixed, (b) instead turning those 24 into winners without changing the 496-trade denominator, and (c) two deliberately impossible high-volatility oracles: drop the entire high-rank closed set, or delete only its losing closed Positions while retaining its winners. For every calculation give original/native observed amounts, included fees/funding, fixed-path winner/closed count and score gaps; do not label the arithmetic as a realizable return because skipping orders would change later slots, quantities, commissions, funding and mark-to-market path. Also solve how many of the existing 284 losers would need to vanish *with all 212 winners unchanged* for an exact 60% closed-trade benchmark. If even a perfect first-bar-loss removal and the high-volatility oracle fail the 60% benchmark, stop treating asset filtering or shock avoidance **alone** as the shortest joint-Goal route; reserve them as possible secondary risk controls and return to source-grounded changes that improve the broad entry/exit population and payoff together. Do not optimize a coin/rank/date cutoff from this already viewed year.

**Fixed-path result, not a backtest:** [D79 native-input-bound arithmetic](results/2026-10-08-d79-goal-headroom.json) verifies all 507 native Position IDs, 496 closed/11 open, 212 winners, H19a's 111,664.46988783-USDT final native equity and the three prior-volatility populations. The exact final-equity threshold for **strictly** more than 20% annualized over 0.9735730594 eligible years is **greater than 119,423.20655419 USDT**; H19a is **7,758.73666636 USDT** short. The 24 first-landmark stopped Positions are only 17 filled bundles and lost **3,572.75565302 USDT native net including fees/funding**. Their impossible clean deletion gives **115,237.22554085 USDT**, **15.68%** annualized fixed-path arithmetic and **212/472 = 44.92%** wins, still below both Goal conditions. Merely flipping all 24 loss labels to wins gives **236/496 = 47.58%**, with no defensible positive replacement PnL. Dropping all 164 high-rank closed Positions gives **45.78%** win and **10.98%** annualized arithmetic; deleting only the 104 high-rank losers while knowing their outcomes in advance gives **54.08%** wins and **33.89%** annualized arithmetic, still below 60% wins despite its impossible hindsight advantage. With all 212 winners unchanged, at least **143 of 284 losers** must vanish to reach 60% win. These computations cannot be promoted to a realizable native account return: deletion would change later capacity, size, fees, funding and market path. The high-rank oracle's annual-return arithmetic is a useful upper fantasy, not an asset policy. Asset class/shock avoidance alone is too narrow for the joint Goal; prioritize source-grounded entry and target structure across the broad opportunity population.

## Source S45 and Diagnostic D80: Al Brooks price-action context before a new native variant

**Source acquisition and boundary:** [The source map](PRICE_ACTION_SOURCE_MAP.md) binds Al Brooks's official [2018 Advanced Gap Techniques slides](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/2018-July-20-CME-Advanced-Gap-Techniques.pdf), [market-cycle manual](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/how-to-trade-price-action-manual/), [strong-leg versus range slides](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/05/Al-Brooks-Webinar-Distinguishing-Strong-Legs-TR-vs-Trends-May-3-2016.pdf), official glossary and course index. Brooks discusses gaps between the bars around a trend bar and a breakout point that remains untested by the pullback; 24/7 crypto need not have a stock-style session-opening gap. He places market-cycle context before isolated candles and emphasizes follow-through to distinguish a breakout from a strong-looking range leg. This source is independent from Ronnie's video interpretation but is not evidence that Ronnie trades by Brooks's exact rules, or that E-mini observations transfer to Binance perpetuals. The currently recorded Ronnie C01/C04/C22 range-location waits make this gap worth testing.

**Registration before reading any Brooks-classified H19a outcome:** Keep H19a's frozen 2,939 native submitted two-tier bundles, 500 filled bundles and 507 Positions, original 37-coin five-minute LAST Catalog identities, registered 60-bar highest B and order source hashes. Reconstruct each bundle's selected A/B at its native submission time from only completed four-hour bars; require native rounded first/deep entry, B target, stop and native order timestamp to agree, otherwise fail closed rather than classify a subset. At B's own completion, compare its close to the **highest high of the preceding 60 completed four-hour bars** (a strict close breakout, no fitted margin). For the immediately following four-hour bar, record bullish follow-through as `close > B.close` and `close > open` only if that bar was already complete before the order submission; otherwise mark it unavailable, never borrow future information. Separately record Brooks's micro-gap geometry as the following bar's low strictly above the preceding bar's high, again only when observed before submission. These are explicitly researcher encodings of source meanings, not Al's unique numeric strategy or three gate variants to pick by score. Report source-state coverage, bundle and native Position count by state, target/stop/time censoring, native closed win/payoff/net PnL with commissions and funding, and month/coin concentration including contrary winners in the weak-breakout state. Treat any read-only subgroup separation as exploratory and selected-fills biased; do not infer a 37-coin annual return or choose a cutoff from the already viewed year. If the strict source encoding is too rare or misclassifies Ronnie C20/C22, examine that *mechanism* once with a separately registered child, not a retrospective threshold sweep. If it produces a coherent broad-population split and source-case acceptance, freeze **one** common Strategy rule before a same-code 37-coin Nautilus paired replay and later delayed/forward qualification. No new price-action engine, indicator language or product preset is authorized.

**Complete native-context read and failed positive-breakout gate:** [D80 summary](results/2026-10-08-d80-brooks-breakout-context.json), SHA-256 `c43f101d0ac1a823c690e4a0629b87f3fbe7da05b94e6a2cbba92164603ff07c`, and [all 2,939 matched bundle records](results/2026-10-08-d80-brooks-breakout-context-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `ac941cfa197cee805c38d7447c296e05aa5819344312cd755e5d574dcac192c4`, bind the registered 37 Nautilus Catalog trees, full 105,222 continuous five-minute LAST bars per coin, D77 native order geometry and D78 original Position paths. The source selector in the current checkout has a different *file* hash after H21/H22 retirement, but every accepted native bundle's first/deep limit, stop, B target and submission time matched a causal selector plan; no unmatched or ambiguous B remained. The one-account native population is unchanged: 500 filled bundles, 507 Positions, 496 closed/11 open, 212 winners and 42.74% closed win.

The strict B **closing breakout** group has **1,428 submitted / 146 filled bundles / 148 closed Positions**, with **54 winners = 36.49%**, payoff **1.572**, and **−1,891.57 USDT** native closed net PnL. The B **did not close beyond** its prior 60-bar high group has **1,511 submitted / 354 filled / 348 closed**, with **158 winners = 45.40%**, payoff **1.632**, and **+13,224.89 USDT** native closed net PnL, including 151 native B-target winners. These are observed fills under the unchanged H19a account, not filtered portfolio returns. The no-close group's win rate is higher in **27/36** coins with both groups represented, but only **6/11** calendar submission months (four reverse, one ties); 158 contrary winners and temporal instability forbid treating the close label as a universal loser detector. The B+1 bar was complete at submission for only **106/2,939** bundles; among closing breakouts, **1,426/1,428** lacked a causal B+1 read, and the remaining two had no bullish follow-through. Therefore Brooks's follow-through cannot be added as an instantaneous H19a gate without **delaying order creation and rerunning native order/fill/account paths**. Only 18 submitted bundles had a causally observed adjacent-bar micro gap; five filled and one closed won, too sparse to validate a gap rule. A strict positive close-breakout filter is contradicted by these exploratory native fills; an inverse filter alone would retain a 45.40% observed win group and cannot close the joint Goal. Do not run a full native variant for either one-bit rule. The second-level source question is how a price-action **range or breakout context changes entry timing and target management**, including the failed-breakout and slow-winner counterexamples, rather than adding a static gap flag. No independent return advantage is established.

## Diagnostic D81: does H19a buy the middle of its own causal range?

**Second-level preregistration before the location read:** Brooks's [official trading-range chapter](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/how-to-trade-manual/trading-ranges/) says context matters and advises against entries in the middle third of a trading range; Ronnie C01/C04/C22 also distinguish edge from middle. D80 showed that a positive closing-breakout gate is the wrong one-bit translation for H19a. Bind the exact D80 bundle/detail files and native hashes above, and reverify each registered Catalog tree and complete four-hour LAST series. At each native order's matched causal signal completion, define the *researcher proxy* range as the high and low of the **last 60 completed four-hour bars including that signal bar**, reusing H19a's existing 60-bar B window rather than selecting a new lookback. Normalize both original native first-tier 50% and deeper-tier 61.8% **planned limit prices** to that range. Freeze lower third as `<=1/3`, middle as `(1/3,2/3)`, upper as `>=2/3`; report outside-range orders separately. The primary population is D80's B-without-closing-breakout group, with the closing-breakout group a disclosed sensitivity, because no-close B is not itself proof of a stable trading range. Count native submitted bundles, filled bundles, closed/open Positions, positive closed Positions, original net PnL/payoff including fees/funding and monthly/coin concentration by **planned first-tier location**; separately report how often the deeper tier actually lies in a lower third when the first is middle. Retain tier-fill ambiguity and all middle-third winners as counterexamples. This remains a selected-fill diagnostic, not an order-deletion or portfolio return. If first-tier midrange dominates and edge states are both sufficiently represented and directionally stronger across time, choose one explicit range-location rule with C01/C04/C20 case acceptance, then one paired native 37-coin run. If edge states are sparse or weak, reject a simple third-based filter and examine a source-supported *post-touch reversal entry* with a delayed native order, rather than sweeping lookbacks or fractional cutoffs on the exposed year.

**Complete read and location-filter rejection:** [D81 summary](results/2026-10-08-d81-brooks-range-location.json), SHA-256 `3f07e19afc59b465e0525946d7597e04687d3a2b8c68533a42ca4b3c54b3fc48`, and [all original native bundles plus causal 60-bar price locations](results/2026-10-08-d81-brooks-range-location-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `6b0ace3f34129944c0e7cb7a04937c4c6da4dab02b88a2ec46141056fdc80eee`, verify every registered 37-coin Nautilus Catalog tree and preserve the D80 population. In the **1,511 no-close-breakout** proxy bundles, planned first-tier buy is in the lower third for **295 submitted / 78 filled / 77 native closed / 32 winners (41.56%)**, original net **+788.11 USDT** and payoff **1.533**. It is in the **middle third** for **1,142 submitted / 250 filled / 246 native closed / 120 winners (48.78%)**, original net **+14,163.21 USDT** and payoff **1.635**. Another 74 first-tier limits are **below** that 60-bar range, with **26 filled / 25 closed / six winners (24.00%)** and **−1,726.43 USDT** native net; this is a separate out-of-range state, not a fitted exclusion. The deeper tier lies in the lower third while the first tier is middle in **421 no-close submitted / 90 filled bundles**; an entire two-tier native bundle cannot be reassigned to a different location by deleting one Position. Across all original bundles, 477 have a unique earliest first-tier fill, 23 have ambiguous same-timestamp earliest fills, and 2,439 are unfilled; no uniquely earliest deeper-tier fill can precede the first limit by construction. The middle-third observed win rate exceeds lower-third in **23/35** coins with both groups, but only **5/11** months; six months reverse. The 120 middle-third winners are direct counterexamples to a blanket Brooks-third exclusion. The analogous close-breakout lower third is **6/22** winners and middle third **48/122**, also not supportive.

This does **not** refute Brooks's advice for an actual trading range: H19a's rolling 60-bar high/low is an arbitrary bounded research proxy and the no-close label does not establish range structure. The tested one-bit “buy only lower third” gate is contradicted in the native selected fills and lacks temporal consistency. Do not implement it as a full native variant or reinterpret selected-fill PnL as filtered-account equity. The next mechanism to register is a **post-touch reversal/second-entry decision with a new causal order time**, supported by Brooks's [own discussion of waiting for a second signal or strong bull breakout against a strong down leg](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/trading-strategies/trading-range-weak-signal-bar-needs-breakout/) and Ronnie's C08/C13 distinction between weak first response and a confirmed bullish candle. It must confront D78's slow-winner counterexamples and native fee/price slippage before an annual score; do not sweep reversal-body thresholds or holding periods on this viewed year.

## Diagnostic D82: can a completed post-touch bull signal support a new native order without discarding the old winners?

**Registration before the post-touch read:** Brooks's [strong-down-leg discussion](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/trading-strategies/trading-range-weak-signal-bar-needs-breakout/) treats a second buy signal or a strong bull breakout as safer than blindly buying the first reversal; Ronnie C08/C13 also wait for a sufficiently convincing completed candle in weak support/break cases. This is a **researcher encoding of one strong bull response**, not a claim that either trader uses the following exact threshold. Bind D77 native bundle/order geometry, D78 native Position first-fill/stop/target/outcome paths, D80 causal breakout context, the 37 registered five-minute LAST Catalog trees, and H19a's native report hashes. Use one opportunity unit per **500 first-filled bundles**; if both tiers open at the same event, retain both native Positions' original outcomes but one touch clock. Treat its earliest native BUY fill as evidence that price reached the original tier; do not invent an earlier touch from OHLC. Only inspect the **single complete UTC four-hour bar containing that first fill**, after it has finished. A candidate bull response means `close > open` **and** `close` is in the upper quarter of that same bar's high-low range; upper quarter is a frozen researcher interpretation of “strong close,” not optimized from this year. Independently report bars whose low touched or crossed the original common native stop and bundles whose original native Position closed before that bar completed. Missing completion near the data end is end-censored. This diagnostic may observe the signal only *at that completed bar close*.

**Causal orderability and rejection gates:** For each signaled bundle, derive a hypothetical next-bar buy-stop trigger **one native tick above the signal bar high** using its native Instrument; require `original stop < trigger < original B target` and record the available target-to-stop R, the original first-tier planned R, the deterioration in price/room, signal delay, and whether price was already at/above B. No new order, fill, fee, funding, Account or annual return is simulated here. Report the signaled/not-signaled/stop-crossed/closed-before-close/end-censored populations, their original native closed winner and target counts including fees/funding, origin volatility and D80 B context, monthly/coin concentration, and especially **original slow eventual winners without a signal** as counterexamples. If an immediate strong bull response covers too few original opportunities or abandons many original winners, do not create a native variant just to score it. A second-level child may then separately register a Brooks-style second signal over a source-justified horizon, preserving this failed first response; do not scan upper-quarter fractions or arbitrary observation bars. If orderability, source-case chronology and winner retention are compelling, freeze one variant's actual native touch-watch, buy-stop, bracket, expiry and risk budget before a BTC/ETH lifecycle pilot and 37-coin one-account paired replay. Any reported old H19a winner conditional on this post-touch state is *selected-fill description*, never the new strategy's win rate.

**Completed D82 result and immediate-confirmation rejection:** [D82 summary](results/2026-10-08-d82-post-touch-bull-response.json), SHA-256 `209f2532bcdc8348f3e792443683ccc933f59085c4ffca41dec2a7dd6df1c7e6`, and [all 500 first-filled native bundle paths](results/2026-10-08-d82-post-touch-bull-response-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `3e30f06d99f4173d193a5f9051551c3412194dcb5c022d3a06146b02135788e1`, match all 507 original Positions, their fees/funding-inclusive PnL, 37 registered Catalog trees and 105,222 contiguous five-minute LAST bars per coin. Only **43/500** bundles have the preregistered completed-bar strong bull response with an orderable one-tick-above-high trigger. These 43 contain **42 original closed Positions**, **12 original winners**, 12 original B-target exits and five D78 slow eventual winners. The **457 without** the response retain **454 original closed Positions, 200 original winners** and **129 D78 slow eventual winners**. Eight first-fill-containing bars crossed the original stop, and eight original Positions closed before their bar completed; none of those eight bars also met the strong response, but both checks remain independently preserved. All 43 signaled cases have `stop < hypothetical trigger < B`; this is geometric orderability, not a submitted native order or new fill. Median confirmation delay after the original first native fill is **195 minutes**. Median target-to-original-stop R at the hypothetical trigger is only **0.967** versus **1.726** at the corresponding original planned first limit; the original risk distance and take-profit would have much less room after waiting. The response appears in only nine submission months and 29 coins, with original closed winners **12/42**, not an observed edge. A direct “wait for the first strong bull bar then buy-stop” full native variant is rejected at this mechanism gate: it would discard too many old winners and deteriorate planned reward room. No annual candidate return or Goal improvement is claimed.

## Diagnostic D83: is a second buy signal after a failed first response a feasible deeper Brooks child?

**Second-level registration before reading later bars:** Brooks's [official glossary](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/price-action-trading-terms-glossary/) defines High 2 as an upward break of a prior bar high after an earlier High 1 and at least one intervening lower high; his [weak-signal article](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/trading-strategies/trading-range-weak-signal-bar-needs-breakout/) says to wait for a second buy signal or strong bull breakout when downside momentum is strong. D82 showed that a strong immediate response is too rare and loses many slow winners. Use the same frozen 500 native first-filled H19a bundle touch clocks, D77 original stop/B target, D78 Position outcomes, D80 causal context and 37 registered LAST Catalog trees. Preserve D82's initial strong-response result unchanged. For each original touch, examine **at most the first five complete four-hour bars strictly after its fill-containing bar**; five is D78's already recorded landmark and one frozen interpretation of Brooks's “few bars,” not a searched horizon. Starting after the partial fill bar avoids assigning pre-touch price action to a post-touch signal. Require a first full post-touch bar whose high exceeds its prior bar's high (High 1), then a subsequent completed bar with a lower high; at that lower-high bar close a hypothetical native buy-stop one instrument tick above its high becomes causally placeable for **the next four-hour bar only**. Count a High 2 *potential trigger* only if that next bar's high reaches the stop price. Use the **first** such sequence and do not restart, choose a later lower high, or sweep bar/body thresholds. Reject a setup if any prior completed bar in the touch-to-trigger path has touched the original stop or B target; if the trigger bar also touches stop/target, mark intrabar sequencing ambiguous rather than assign an outcome. Keep end-of-data and original native early-closure flags separate.

**Decision and limits:** Report opportunities with causal order placement, observable next-bar trigger, target-to-stop R, delay, retained original native winners/targets and D78 slow winners versus those omitted, plus coin/month/context concentration and contrary failures. This is a **read-only geometry and coverage** screen, not a high-2 Strategy or a replayed win rate. A potential trigger seen in a completed bar is not proof of a native fill or execution order under the candidate. If it still covers too few of the 212 original winners or compresses R, reject this second-entry translation and examine market-cycle/target management as a different source-backed branch. If it covers a meaningful part of the broad population and passes C08/C13/C20 chronology, preregister **one** native Strategy implementation with order lifecycle, fee/funding and capital effects, then pilot and 37-coin one-account paired replay. Do not read several horizons or choose the best definition by this exposed year's PnL.

**High 2 read and stand-alone capacity decision:** [D83 summary](results/2026-10-08-d83-high2-post-touch.json), SHA-256 `2c94f7b360c38418d8317f1a771c5f00a586ea757dca30466ed7e82d041a90a0`, and [all 500 native touch paths](results/2026-10-08-d83-high2-post-touch-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `57132f9b7b498df70115622219b3a4ba7e087813d0e3b1f178976725fc9d796d`, verify the same 37 Catalog trees and registered original inputs. **265/500** opportunities reach a causally placeable hypothetical stop order after a High 1 and a later lower-high completed bar; only **108/500** next bars' highs reach their one-tick-above-high trigger without same-bar old-stop/B-target OHLC ambiguity. The remaining main states include **157** setup orders whose next bar did not reach the trigger, **137** with no lower high inside the fixed five complete bars, **48** with prior stop/B invalidation and **48** without an in-window High 1; two are end-censored. The 108 potential-trigger bundles contain **101 original native closed Positions / 56 original winners (55.45%) / 52 original B-target wins / seven original open**, plus **35** of D78's 134 slow eventual winners. Their original selected native net PnL is **+9,158.05 USDT** including original fees/funding; it is not candidate PnL because a later buy stop changes price, fills, sizing and cashflows. The 137 no-lower-high cases alone retain **68** original winners, a strong-trend counterexample to using High 2 as the only entry path.

At the 108 potential-trigger prices, geometric target-to-original-stop R averages **1.259** and has median **1.201**, before slippage. A deliberately optimistic stand-alone capacity illustration assumes all 108 trigger events become native fills at the trigger, every trade uses the full original **25 bp** account risk, each loss is exactly −1R, gains are exactly each trigger's B-target R, every opportunity has a 60% win probability, and fees/funding/notional caps/compounding vanish. Its expected fixed-initial-capital period gain is only **9.60%**, versus the registered **19.423%** period gain needed for strictly more than 20% annualized. At 25 bp and those same heroic assumptions it would need **76.11%** wins; at 60% wins it would need **50.60 bp** per event before costs and correlations. These are *not* a native risk recommendation, a confidence interval or a qualified edge. High 2 alone is too sparse and price-degraded to justify a full native variant under the existing risk contract, despite its positive selected old PnL. The source-supported alternative in the same Brooks article is a strong bull breakout after the touch; test that branch once, including overlap with High 2, before abandoning post-touch entry work.

## Diagnostic D84: does a later strong bull breakout complement High 2 without cherry-picking the same year?

**Pre-registered second source branch:** Brooks's [own weak-signal discussion](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/trading-strategies/trading-range-weak-signal-bar-needs-breakout/) gives two alternatives when a first reversal against strong selling is unreliable: a second buy signal **or** a strong bull breakout. D82 tested an immediate strong response in the fill-containing bar and found it too rare; D83 tested High 2 in five subsequent complete bars and found it too narrow. Keep the same D82 500 original native filled-bundle touch clocks, D83 five complete four-hour bars strictly after the partial fill bar, 37 verified Catalog trees, original native stop/B target, and all fees/funding-inclusive old Position outcomes. Reuse D82's **exact frozen** strong-close geometry `close > open` and `close >= low + 0.75*(high-low)`; add Brooks's breakout condition `close > prior completed four-hour bar high`. Do not choose a different body fraction or lookback by annual PnL. In order to allow a next-bar buy-stop inside the same five-bar horizon, search the **first four** complete bars after touch and take the **first** signal only. At its close, derive one native tick above its high as a hypothetical next-bar buy-stop, active only during the next complete four-hour bar. Reject if the touch-containing bar or any already completed bar through the signal touched the original stop or B target; preserve this conservative partial-bar ambiguity. Mark trigger-bar old-stop/B-target touches as sequencing-ambiguous, not wins. Count original native closures before signal separately.

**Frozen comparison and next gate:** Compare this strong-breakout branch with D83 High 2 at the **bundle** unit: counts of causal order placement, potential next-bar trigger, target-to-stop R and original winners/slow winners, then their unique union. If both branches produce a potential trigger for the same bundle, retain the earlier hypothetical order time for union geometry, with High 2 winning an exact tie; never count the bundle twice. Report by coin/month and D80 B context, original target/stop/time censoring, and a labeled 25-bp/60%-win fixed-capital illustration using the unique union's geometric target R, not a candidate account return. If the union still cannot plausibly bridge the joint Goal under the current 25-bp budget without a much higher win rate or risk multiplier, close this bounded post-touch entry branch and move to a source-grounded target/account-allocation mechanism. If the union has adequate coverage and source chronology, register **one** actual native Strategy and same-code paired shared-account replay; do not select the better-looking branch after seeing this year's subgroup PnL. Preserve all D82/D83 failures and delayed/forward qualification requirements.

**Complete two-branch read and bounded promotion:** [D84 summary](results/2026-10-08-d84-later-bull-breakout.json), SHA-256 `3f88fb41510571ec61fcec6f2192d5dcd36ad6afbdd08b13693626c7fdecf8ed`, and [all 500 original bundle paths](results/2026-10-08-d84-later-bull-breakout-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `4b82a42796db07f4333bc15c25fac40cd388d755c3dc278fff0a604529396df1`, bind D82/D83 and all registered native Catalog trees. The later strong-breakout branch alone has **148** potential next-bar triggers; High 2 has **108**, with **33** overlapping, leaving **223 unique** potential triggers. Earlier hypothetical order time allocates 91 to High 2 and 132 to strong breakout. The unique selected cohort contains **214 original closed Positions, 111 original winners (51.87%), 64 D78 slow eventual winners**, and original native selected net **+11,862.89 USDT**. These are **old H19a fills/PnL**, not outcomes after new orders. The unique geometric target-R sum is **261.281**. Under the same explicitly optimistic no-cost, perfect-fill, exact −1R, full-25bp-risk illustration, a 60% win probability gives **16.89% fixed-initial-capital period gain** versus the **19.423%** period threshold; reaching it would require **62.09%** wins at 25bp or **28.75bp** risk at 60%, *before* fees, slippage, notional caps, correlated losses and changed account paths. The mechanism therefore has enough breadth to justify **one** actual native 25-bp test, but has no established edge or sizing license. The D84 union uses future observed highs only as a coverage bound: the real Strategy must submit a stop after a completed setup bar, allow an unfilled one-bar attempt to expire, and process later source-branch signals causally. No hindsight choice between branches is allowed.

## Candidate H23a: native post-touch Brooks High 2 or strong breakout entry

**Registered before Strategy edits and any economic replay:** This is an explicitly labeled **Brooks-informed research child of H19a**, not Ronnie's exact 50%/61.8% resting-limit execution. Preserve the same 37 Binance USDT perpetual Catalog trees, five-minute LAST/MARK, funding settlements, one native 100,000-USDT margin account, 2025-10-07 input start, 2025-10-17 trade eligibility and 2026-10-07 08:30 UTC end; preserve H19a's broad confirmed A/B selector, 60-bar B and 180-bar A clocks, 50%/61.8% source price levels, 76.4%-minus-0.25-prior-ATR protective stop, B first obstacle, 180-four-hour-bar plan/hold horizon, 25-bp *total per setup* original-stop-risk budget and 5% coin-notional cap. Change the **entry policy as one indivisible hypothesis**: register one unfilled watch after the H19a plan, wait for an actual subsequent native five-minute LAST low to touch the first 50% tier (which also admits a deeper 61.8% touch), and never place the original resting limits. Ignore the partly pre-touch four-hour bar for signal detection. Over the next **five complete four-hour bars**, track the first High 1 (`high > previous high`), then the first lower-high completed bar; after that bar closes, permit one one-tick-above-high native **STOP_MARKET** parent for only the next four-hour bar (High 2 path). Independently, in the first four complete bars after touch, the first bar with `close > open`, `close >= low + 0.75*(high-low)` and `close > previous high` permits the same one-bar buy stop (strong-breakout path). Do not retrospectively wait to see whether that next bar reaches the trigger. At each completed signal, if no prior entry is open/pending, submit the earlier available source-branch order, breaking a same-time tie toward High 2. If it does not fill during that next four-hour bar, let native GTD expiration/cancel resolve before a later signal from the *other*, as-yet-unused branch can submit once. Do not re-arm either branch, extend the five-bar window, sweep the strong-close fraction or add another tier. If either native stop-entry fills, cancel competing entry attempts, keep its native OTO protective stop and B take-profit, and do not add to the Position. A new higher B supersedes an unfilled watched plan; an open protected Position retains its original stop/B. Before any entry, native minute observations of the original stop or B after touch invalidate the watch; overlapping events in the same touch minute are not ordered by four-hour OHLC. A signal or trigger with `stop >= trigger` or `trigger >= B` is rejected. A new entry is one native bracket, sized by the same total 25-bp stop-risk and 5% notional cap; it is not two phantom tiers or a parallel account.

**Required gates and paired decision:** Freeze source/Strategy/runner hashes; exercise synthetic closed-bar chronology, first touch, no-touch, strong-breakout, High 2, same-time precedence, one-bar unfilled expiry, later branch, supersession, stop/B invalidation, immediate same-bar stop, target and time exit with native Nautilus orders/Position protection. The C08/C13 source cases support waiting for a completed strong response; C20 supports tier *locations* and outside invalidation but also permits prospective resting limits, so H23a deliberately differs on order timing and may not claim Ronnie fidelity there. Run a BTC/ETH Catalog pilot only as an execution integrity gate, then one complete H23a 37-coin shared-account native replay and a same-code H19a paired control, requiring exact native H19a order/fill/equity parity against the published rc3 frozen control. Independently audit actual parent/child OTO topology, zero denied/rejected or unprotected open positions, native stop/target/expiry, commissions, funding and Account balances. Report annualized net Portfolio return, closed native Position win, realized payoff, Sharpe365, daily-close drawdown, count and open censoring; compare with H19a by paired ISO-week blocks using the registered 5,000-draw seed 20261008, disclose this as one among the accumulated exposed-year attempts, and do not promote a point-estimate win without delayed/forward qualification. If H23a misses the joint Goal, diagnose whether scarcity, worse entry/R, or execution/capital risk caused the shortfall; a later risk-budget child requires independently justified account-level bounds rather than multiplying 25bp merely to force the annual percentage.

## Diagnostic D85: does the H23a shortfall come from lost breadth or worse entry geometry?

**Pre-registered before matched-bundle diagnostic reads:** Use only the frozen H23a and same-code H19a native orders/fills/Positions above and the previously sealed D82 original 500 filled-bundle paths. Separately report all-run native closures, average winner and loss, fees/funding, open censoring and order funnel without assuming matching cohorts are causal treatment effects. Break out by coin and calendar month only descriptively, not to choose assets. The read cannot reconstruct the H23a plan's first touch from order reports or claim counterfactual PnL for missed orders. If sparse matching or changed account opportunity set dominates, retain it as a limit and use a Strategy trace in a new preregistered run rather than force links. If later price reduces R and breadth jointly, close this specific delayed-entry timing child; then move to one source-grounded gap-persistence/target mechanism on a pre-defined candidate, with costs, stop and survival tradeoffs assessed before another full native replay. No risk increase, branch threshold scan or per-coin selection is authorized by D85.

## Diagnostic D86: after a native B target, does a Brooks micro gap persist often enough to justify a protected runner?

**Source and preregistration before any post-B Catalog read:** Brooks's official [CME Advanced Gap Techniques](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/2018-July-20-CME-Advanced-Gap-Techniques.pdf), slides 3-5, warns most gap attempts fail and defines a bullish micro gap by nonoverlap between the **bar before** and **bar after** a breakout bar. Slides 10-13 discuss a pullback holding the breakout point as a possible measuring gap and caution that profit taking may occur at a measured move. Translate only the continuous-market bar-relative gap, not a session-open jump. Freeze the same 37 H19a Catalog trees, exact paired rc3 H19a Positions and D82 per-position native B-target close causes, one 100,000-USDT account, and the original first-tier price/old stop/B from native orders. At each original H19a **first B-target fill per filled bundle**, identify its containing complete four-hour LAST breakout bar, immediately previous complete four-hour bar and immediately following complete four-hour bar. A bullish micro gap is `following.low > preceding.high`; its status is knowable only when the following bar completes. Separately count target fills whose containing minute or path to that decision also touched the old protective stop; classify same-minute/same-four-hour ambiguous cases rather than calling a runner survivor. Use native five-minute LAST only to establish stop survival until the gap decision, not to simulate fills or fees.

**Single bounded outcome read and gate:** For each stop-surviving observable gap/no-gap case, from the **next** complete four-hour bar onward count the first crossing over the next **20 complete four-hour bars** of an illustrative `B + (original_first_tier_price − old_stop)` continuation level versus the old stop, separate within-bar dual-touch ambiguity and end censoring. Report incidence, observable survivors, native original target PnL, continuation-before-stop counts and coin/month distributions for gap and no-gap cases; no model PnL, new orders or promoted return. This geometric +1R level is one registered capacity benchmark, not an optimized take-profit. Require at least **30 observable gap cases** and a clear source-consistent continuation difference before registering a native partial-B-take-profit/runner child with unchanged total risk, explicit OTO protection and same-code paired replay. If prevalence or separation is inadequate, close this micro-gap target extension and inspect broader pre-entry trend/range context or account allocation without sweeping gap/body thresholds on this exposed year. All later economic claims still require fees, funding, full native account and delayed/forward qualification.

**Completed source-clock read, with confounder retained:** [D86 summary](results/2026-10-08-d86-b-target-micro-gap.json), SHA-256 `ca985b27064917f5890f21174856d179c1c6cdc031d63812aeef50ff496ffaf0`, and [all native B-exit cases](results/2026-10-08-d86-b-target-micro-gap-cases.json.gz), SHA-256 `373a8beb3b8180ed7763fb0d0a18adecf5755f67b18b7158ffb2ff6af8aa967d`, verify every registered 37-coin Catalog tree and the frozen H19a/rc3 reports. There are **199** first native B-target exits, all observable through the following complete four-hour bar without an old-stop touch on or after the B target minute under native LAST minute bars. The source's surrounding-bar micro gap occurs in **115**, versus **84** no-gap cases. In the gap group **24** already touched the illustrative B+1 first-tier-R continuation level *before* the gap could be observed, versus **1** in no-gap. Among only the **91** and **83** remaining fresh cases, **51/91 (56.0%)** versus **30/83 (36.1%)** later reached that level before the old stop over the next 20 complete four-hour bars; the rest include 40 versus 49 unresolved at 20 bars and 0 versus 4 old-stop-first events. This is a **geometric continuation read after old exit**, not a runner fill, fee/funding read or new account return. A descriptive post-read cross-tab shows the difference is entangled with whether the breakout-containing candle closed above B: fresh above-B-close cases are 38/59 versus 6/25, while at/below-B-close cases are 13/32 versus 24/58. Because the interaction was noticed after this year's outcomes, **do not add a B-close gate** to the next economic candidate. The registered ≥30 prevalence gate passes and the source-consistent unadjusted separation justifies **one** native gap-only runner child; it does not independently prove an edge or authorize threshold search.

## Candidate H24a: native half-B/half-runner exit after source-defined micro-gap test

**Registered before Strategy code and economic replay:** Keep H19a's exact broad A/B selector, 50% and 61.8% resting-limit prices, old 76.4%-minus-0.25 prior ATR stop, 180-four-hour-bar entry/hold horizon, native LAST/MARK/funding Catalogs, same fixed 37 coins and one native 100,000-USDT account, 25bp *total* original-stop risk per selected setup and 5% coin-notional cap. At native order submission, split **each original tier's quantity** into two native LIMIT-parent OTO brackets: half (rounding remainder assigned to B share) retains the original B target, and half is a runner with the *same old stop* and a single fixed target `B + (rounded first-tier entry − rounded old stop)`. Total per-tier quantity, stop-risk and notional remain no larger than H19a; reject the whole tier bundle if either share violates native quantity or notional minimum. Each of the four parents retains the original 180-bar GTD. On the **first actual native B take-profit fill**, cancel any still-unfilled sibling entries. The B-fill-containing four-hour bar is only partly post-exit; at the close of the immediately following full four-hour LAST bar, compare its low with the high of the bar immediately before the containing breakout bar. If `following.low <= preceding.high` (no source-defined bullish micro gap) and a runner Position remains, cancel its native exits and flatten the residual quantity by a native market order. If `following.low > preceding.high`, retain the runner's native B+1-first-tier-R take-profit and original stop until either fills or the original 180-bar hold exit. If the runner reaches its target or stop before the gap observation, keep that native outcome; never use the future gap status to reverse it. Do not add a B-close, body, coin, volatility, risk multiplier or gap-age gate based on D86's exposed-year cross-tab. This changes **only target allocation and the post-B condition exit** as one bounded H19a child; it is not Ronnie fidelity evidence.

**Required gates and decision:** Exercise native synthetic full/partial entry, two B target fills, early runner target/stop, same-bar B/stop race, cancellation of unfilled sibling entries, no-gap market exit, persistent-gap runner, 180-bar time exit, quantity minima and OTO protection. BTC/ETH is an integrity pilot, then one complete 37-coin H24a shared-account replay and same-code H19a control with exact rc3 report parity. Audit all four parent/child groups, total 25bp risk and 5% notional cap at submission, no denied/rejected or unprotected open Positions, native commissions/funding and Account equity. Report annualized net return, native closed-position win, realized payoff, Sharpe365, daily-close drawdown, trade count/open censoring, and paired ISO-week 5,000-draw seed 20261008 against H19a. Judge H24a against the **joint** >20% annual and near-60%-win Goal, disclosing multiple attempts and requiring delayed/forward confirmation before promotion. If it fails, decompose whether the runner's extra target PnL, no-gap exit, extra native costs, min-size skips or changed slot occupancy caused it; do not tune the runner share or B+R distance on this year.

**Native implementation and precision repair:** The BTC/ETH pilot exercised native B/runner target fills, persistent-gap hold, no-gap market exits and time exit with zero denials/rejections; its native 536-bracket topology passed. The first full-year run produced a **failed** topology audit: one PEPE tier's binary-float half split differed by two native size increments, violating the registered half-and-remainder rule even though the orders were accepted. That **+12.13% preliminary point estimate is invalid** as H24a evidence. The only repair replaced binary float splitting with `Decimal` integer multiples of the native Instrument `size_increment`, assigning the remainder to B; a separate annual PEPE Catalog pilot then passed. The corrected full-year replay was rerun once. This was an execution-precision repair, not a changed source hypothesis or selected economic parameter.

## Diagnostic D87: why did the source-consistent B-runner geometry barely change the native account?

**Registered before causal-branch outcome aggregation:** Bind the corrected H24a and same-code H19a summaries, native order/fill/Position/account reports and SHA-256, passing four-bracket/gap-clock/parity audits, and the unchanged D86 Catalog trees.
