Report count, native PnL and payoff for these lifecycle categories, plus max risk/notional, min-size skips and positions/trade-count changes. For an old/new plan comparison, require unique exact `(instrument, stop, B, source order ts_init)` on both sides; report unmatched and ambiguous keys and never transfer an old result to a new order. Break down by coin/month only as descriptive custody. The primary question is whether the 76 no-gap flattenings, 33 early runner-target fill events, 82 persistent-gap holds or changed slot occupancy explain why D86's post-exit geometric difference became only +0.13 annual points and a lower win rate. This is a mechanism audit, not a search across runner shares, B+R distances or B-close subgroups. If no large causal defect emerges, de-prioritize this exact partial-runner child and shift to a pre-entry market-cycle/asset-behavior mechanism that can improve both win probability and opportunity-adjusted return; any later economic child needs a new source case, fixed rule and delayed validation.

**Read-only result and failure mechanism:** [D87 frozen native lifecycle](results/2026-10-08-d87-h24a-lifecycle.json), SHA-256 `cd7e40643e31b0de5147a652af3bf5f116e040669c7324c5d61d564927ca4ebd`, binds the H24a summary/orders/fills/Positions, same-code H19a summary and 37-coin Catalog identity by SHA. Of H24a's **488 native Positions**, 477 are closed and 11 censored open. The **306 Positions without any B fill** include 295 closed, only **13 winners**, with native closed realized PnL **−51,078.78 USDT**; all 11 open Positions are here. All **182 Positions with a real B fill** closed positive, split into 24 exiting before the gap decision (**+8,709.19 USDT**), 82 persistent-gap (**+30,260.34 USDT**) and 76 no-gap (**+23,569.30 USDT**). These are *selected* native Position groups, not a future B classifier. There were **23 Positions** with an early runner-target fill (33 fill events), 85 Positions ultimately closed by runner targets, 271 by stops and 121 by native market orders; **76** market exits were no-gap flattenings and **45** other native market exits occurred at the 180-bar hold limit. Max observed Strategy-submitted original-stop risk was **0.24999997%** of native equity and notional **4.99999963%**, with zero minimum-share skips. Native closed realized Position PnL is **+11,460.05 USDT**; period-end Account equity and fee/funding totals remain the authoritative portfolio result in H24a's summary/economics receipts.

**Exact old/new custody:** Of **2,661** H24a and **2,939** H19a native bundles, **2,513** unique `(instrument, stop, B, order ts_init)` keys are shared, 148 new-only and 426 old-only; there are zero repeated bundle keys. Seven bundles on each side split into multiple native Positions during same-timestamp execution, so D87 sums *reported Position PnL within each exact bundle* before a descriptive paired comparison. The **430 fully closed matched bundles** show H24a minus H19a native reported PnL **−114.64 USDT** without B fill, **+1,781.64** before the gap decision, **+3,028.84** for persistent gaps, and **−2,272.89** for no-gap market exits: total **+2,422.95 USDT**. The **26 H24a** and **59 H19a** other closed Positions contribute **+1,193.83** and **+3,490.06 USDT** respectively, offsetting **−2,296.23 USDT** of that difference; total closed native Position PnL difference is only **+126.73 USDT**, consistent with the **+127.57-USDT** Account equity difference after open valuation. Thus the native no-gap exit *cuts* relative gains from old B exits and the altered order/slot path offsets most matched runner gain. The paired Position cohorts are descriptive and not a causal treatment estimate because one shared account changes subsequent opportunity selection. H24a is not improved by simply scanning runner shares or targets on this exposed year. The dominant 295 pre-B closed Positions, 282 of them nonpositive point toward pre-entry price-action context and actual reversal probability, not a late post-B extension rule; they do **not** license hindsight exclusion of losers.

## Diagnostic D88: distinguish a strong A-to-B leg from a range leg before planning a pullback

**Registered before any D88 outcome read:** Al Brooks's [strong-leg versus trading-range presentation](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/05/Al-Brooks-Webinar-Distinguishing-Strong-Legs-TR-vs-Trends-May-3-2016.pdf) says an apparent strong leg inside a range can fail as a trend; his [price-action manual](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/how-to-trade-price-action-manual/) places market-cycle context before an isolated candle pattern. D80's one-bit 60-bar closing breakout gate and D81's first-tier location proxy did not work; D87 found the loss mass occurs *before* B. Use frozen H19a **native submitted plan and fill times**, verified 37-coin four-hour Catalog bars and native Position outcomes to construct only **causally available at order submission** A-to-B leg measures: directional efficiency `abs(B close − A close) / sum(abs(close-to-close change))` over the complete A-to-B bars, one-bar high/low overlap frequency, and whether B is above the preceding 60 complete-bar high. Report missing/short legs and already-occupied positions explicitly. Compare the fixed bottom/middle/top thirds of the *measure's logical [0,1] scale* for efficiency and overlap, not sample quantiles; cross-tab each with native fill probability, pre-B stop versus B completion, net realized Position PnL, counts, coin/month stability and 11 open censored Positions. **Do not** combine dimensions or choose a profitable cutoff in this diagnostic. Inspect low-efficiency winners and high-efficiency losers as mandatory counterexamples. A later one-rule native Strategy child requires a stable direction across time/coins, sufficient opportunity capacity toward the joint Goal, a prospectively fixed threshold with source rationale and delayed/forward validation. If these measures fail, study an independently sourced market-cycle or asset-behavior mechanism rather than tune their cutoffs on this year.

**D88 source-bound result and decision:** [D88 summary](results/2026-10-08-d88-brooks-leg-context.json), SHA-256 `0abffaf16fba6a6264d72560f3ced8bbc4c4e1e43dc27631fa32ee918a4eb28c`, and [all 2,939 native bundles with A/B provenance](results/2026-10-08-d88-brooks-leg-context-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `fb6d92e1330e9ebd92ba1da725849ee78e9f77d9d47a6bc245feb73d407299fa`, bind the same-code H19a summary, orders, Positions, prior D77/D80 outcome receipts and all 37 Nautilus Catalog trees. They exactly recover **500 filled bundles, 507 Positions, 496 closed, 212 winners, 11 open**; all 2,939 plans have a unique causal A/B source, the shortest complete leg is 9 bars, none is submitted while another same-coin native Position is open, and they verify that none of the 266 native stop-closed Positions had any earlier B target fill. Directional efficiency on its fixed logical thirds: lower **2,129 submitted / 385 filled / 384 closed / 162 wins = 42.19% / +7,660.39 USDT**; middle **605 / 93 / 90 / 42 = 46.67% / +4,045.46**; upper **205 / 22 / 22 / 8 = 36.36% / −372.52**. The upper group has **14 nonwinners**, the lower retains **162 winners**. Only one coin and two submission months have at least three closed upper and lower cases each; no stable cross-asset/time claim follows. Every one of 2,939 plans falls in the *upper* third of the boolean adjacent high/low-overlap frequency: a 24/7 four-hour-bar overlap occurrence is essentially saturated in this A/B population, so the coarse binary frequency cannot distinguish range from trend. The unchanged 60-bar closing breakout comparison repeats D80's failed direction. No economic Strategy child is justified by these measurements; the fixed-third PnL are selected **old native Position** results, not an executable filtered-account return.

## Diagnostic D89: does overlap depth and pullback structure distinguish a trend leg from a range leg?

**Second-level mechanism registered before D89 outcome aggregation:** D88's boolean overlap metric saturated; it did **not** test Brooks's statement that trend/channel/range differ by *how much* bars overlap, how often pullbacks occur, and whether pullbacks are deep. The author's [2016 slides 8 and 17](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/05/Al-Brooks-Webinar-Distinguishing-Strong-Legs-TR-vs-Trends-May-3-2016.pdf) distinguish a tight channel with shallow 1–3-bar pullbacks from a broad channel/range with deeper, longer, more overlapping pullbacks. Retain D88's exact frozen 2,939 H19a native bundles, A/B timestamps, outcomes and Catalog identity. Before looking at outcome bins, compute three *separate* causally available A-to-B descriptors: the mean per-pair overlap **depth** `max(0, min(highs)−max(lows))/min(two bar ranges)` on [0,1]; the fraction of completed A-to-B bars with close below open (bear-bar frequency); and maximum close-to-close pullback from a prior running high divided by the A/B high-low span, clipped only at 1 and with zero-span marked undefined. Fix bottom/middle/top bins at 1/3 and 2/3 of each logical [0,1] scale. Report counts, original native fill/stop/B outcome, native net PnL, 11 open censored, month/coin direction, sparse bins and high/low counterexamples for each descriptor. Do not combine three descriptors, fit a score, alter risk, select a cutoff or claim filtered shared-account return. If a descriptor lacks breadth or stable direction, reject that simple proxy while retaining the broader source mechanism for a genuinely distinct, prospectively registered second child; any economic rule still requires one native 37-coin paired replay and delayed/forward validation.

**D89 read and deeper failure mechanism:** [D89 complete summary](results/2026-10-08-d89-brooks-pullback-context.json), SHA-256 `22db0796fca4b35dbe7cf3dd7a9abe5b30d368c37e0728aef430cc9c3a5f792f`, and [all source-bound bundles](results/2026-10-08-d89-brooks-pullback-context-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `e3d773bcce49e84c109200f8172e2801ea69249408d804865a003c7861630439`, preserve 2,939 H19a plans and 496 native closed Positions. Mean adjacent high/low **overlap depth** is still too saturated: lower 0 plans, middle only **83 submitted / 12 closed / 5 wins**, upper **2,856 submitted / 484 closed / 207 wins**. Bear-bar frequency has **no upper-third plans**, while lower contains **327 submitted / 31 closed / 15 wins**, middle 2,612 / 465 / 197; it likewise cannot form a broad discriminating extreme. The *maximum close pullback fraction* does separate: lower **1,652 submitted / 261 filled / 260 closed / 99 wins = 38.08%, +927.23 USDT**; middle **1,082 / 197 / 195 / 91 = 46.67%, +6,636.93**; upper **205 / 42 / 41 / 22 = 53.66%, +3,769.16**. Direction of upper versus lower win rate is higher in **2/3** comparable coins and **3/6** comparable months, with 19 upper-third nonwinners and 99 lower-third winners. This is the *opposite* of a simple “shallow pullback means safe trend buy” gate in the existing H19a limit-entry population; the upper-third group has only 42 native filled bundles, too sparse to infer a repeatable 37-coin annual advantage. The middle-plus-upper boundary was **not pre-registered as an economic candidate** and cannot be selected for a 37-coin replay on this already exposed year. A later child must distinguish whether the deeper A-to-B retracements represent a repeatable Brooks range/support location or a coincidental selected-fill mix. Retain the market-cycle direction, discard these scalar overlap and bear-bar proxies, and require a source-grounded **structural** range/pullback definition before any next native candidate.

## Diagnostic D90: were A and B tests of previously confirmed swing support and resistance?

**Registered before structural outcome aggregation:** Brooks's [price-action account of support/resistance](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/price-action/what-is-price-action-6-aspects/) explains drawing a line after prior reversals and observing tests; his [trading-range manual](https://www.brookstradingcourse.com/how-to-trade-manual/trading-ranges/) distinguishes an active range's edges from a strong leg inside it. D81's lower third of a raw 60-bar high/low box failed to establish an *active* range; D88/D89 scalar A-to-B overlap and efficiency also failed to establish structure. Use the same frozen H19a 2,939 native submitted bundles, D88 uniquely identified A/B times, same 37 verified Nautilus Catalog trees and original native Position outcomes. Before looking at D90 outcome, define one **structural test** using the Strategy's existing eight-bars-each-side confirmed swing order and 180-complete-four-hour-bar history: a prior confirmed pivot high before B counts as a B-resistance test only if its high is within **one prior 14-bar Wilder ATR** of the rounded B high; a prior confirmed pivot low before A counts as an A-support test only if its low is within one such ATR of the rounded A low. The tested A/B itself never counts as its own prior test, and every pivot must be confirmed by the order-submission clock. One ATR is a fixed researcher tolerance for price-scale normalization, **not a Brooks numeric prescription**, and must not be scanned. Classify all plans into neither / A support only / B resistance only / both; report test counts, span, native fill, B versus stop/time closure, fees/funding-inclusive native realized PnL, 11 open censoring, coin/month support, sparse groups and winner/loser counterexamples. The question is whether an *already tested range boundary* provides more useful context than D81's window thirds and D89's pullback size. This is only a read-only mechanism; do not select a profitable subgroup as a new Strategy gate or infer filtered portfolio equity from old Positions. A later native rule would need a single source-grounded threshold, adequate breadth toward the joint Goal, same-account paired replay and delayed/forward qualification.

**Structural read and counterexamples:** [D90 summary](results/2026-10-08-d90-brooks-structural-range.json), SHA-256 `604031664a3abb04252571ccb5c660335ba800c49ab40a408da3b5eaef463985`, and [2,939 causal plans](results/2026-10-08-d90-brooks-structural-range-bundles.json.gz), SHA-256 `5e380f7a3d56b4c359f80077595ce3331ab4a3996d6305ab64bd1adf78947d89`, preserve 500 actual filled bundles, 507 Positions, 496 closed, 212 winners and 11 open. **Neither** prior test: 965 submitted / 161 filled / 163 closed / 56 wins = **34.36%**, native closed PnL **−2,433.58 USDT**. **Prior A support only:** 626 / 104 / 98 / 55 = **56.12%**, payoff **1.860**, native closed PnL **+12,598.66 USDT**, but **9 open censored** Positions. **Prior B resistance only:** 933 / 177 / 176 / 75 = **42.61%**, +589.64 USDT. **Both:** 415 / 58 / 59 / 26 = **44.07%**, +578.60 USDT, with 32 nonwinning bundles. A-support-only wins over neither in **8/9** comparable coins and **6/8** comparable submission months (at least three closed in each group); B-resistance-only 14/23 coins and 6/11 months, both 3/5 and 5/8. This is a source-grounded structural distinction that survived the coarse D81 and D88/D89 failures descriptively, but a selected subgroup on a heavily exposed year. Its old Position PnL cannot be a new Account result, and 104 old filled bundles may be a capacity limit. A native candidate can test actual opportunity reallocation and open valuation, but **cannot independently validate** the selected feature on this same year.

## Candidate H25a: native prior-A-support pullback without prior-B-resistance test

**Registered after D90 read, before code or replay:** One explicitly *exploratory* Brooks-informed child of H19a, with the same fixed 37 coins, one 100,000-USDT native shared margin account, 2025-10-07 through 2026-10-07 Catalog, 50%/61.8% original LIMIT tiers, old native OTO stop/B target, 180-bar pending/hold time, total 25bp original-stop risk, 5% coin-notional cap, fees/funding and no live account. Require at least one earlier confirmed pivot low within one ATR of rounded A low and **no** earlier confirmed pivot high within one ATR of rounded B high. No future bar, fill or Position result may participate. If false, suppress this candidate's parent OTO order bundles; retain otherwise identical parent/child prices, quantities, expiry and lifecycle. This is one source-derived state, **not** a sweep among D90's four groups or ATR/pivot values. B-resistance absence is part of the registered source mechanism: prior high can be an obstacle to a B retest, while tested A support can justify a long pullback. The chosen conjunction was noticed on exposed D90 outcomes, so label any same-year gain exploratory regardless of point estimate.

The previous H24a synthetic race suite remains incomplete; H25a must not inherit its runner mechanism.

## Diagnostic D91: is H25a's joint-Goal shortfall primarily opportunity breadth or account reservation?

**Registered before post-H25 path attribution:** Bind H25a and exact same-code H19a native summary/order/fill/Position/account reports and SHA, both native protection audits, the H25 completed-bar decision audit and frozen D90 Catalog identity. At native **submitted bundle** unit, match old/new only by unique exact `(instrument, stop, B, original source signal time)` when the source clock is available; where old/new order submission moved, report source-key ambiguity and keep opportunity paths separate. At native Position unit, report closed/open counts, wins, target/stop/time closures, native realized PnL and fee/funding, and unique comparable old/new bundles without inventing PnL for suppressed orders. Quantify the **time-weighted native Account locked/total fraction** between trade start and end from `account.csv`, with p50/p95/max and fraction of elapsed time below 1% locked; this measures *native margin reservation*, not total notional exposure, stop risk or a right to scale leverage. Compare H25a's 3,802 plan decisions, 656 accepted, 655 submitted, 190 filled parents and 102 closed against H19a's funnel and disclose censored opens. The question is whether 394 fewer closures, missed source opportunities, changed funding or genuinely constrained capital explains why improved win/payoff/Sharpe still gives only +13.13% annual. No risk multiplier, allocation optimizer, per-coin filter or same-year threshold sweep may be selected from this diagnostic. If capacity is the limiting mechanism, the next source-derived research should create *additional* structurally supported opportunities or test account reallocation with predeclared risk bounds, then one native paired replay and delayed/forward validation.

**D91 exact source and Account read:** [D91 frozen report](results/2026-10-08-d91-h25a-capacity.json), SHA-256 `632f8fd4fc5c1dc4dacf226a990117762e08830a39b35bacfc8313f67ae9d1f8`, binds both complete native report sets and passing audits. Of H25a's **655 native bundles**, **626** have a unique matching original H19a `(coin, stop, B, source signal)` bundle, **29** are new H25a-only source opportunities released under the changed account path; **2,313** H19a bundles do not occur in H25a, zero ambiguous source keys. The 626 exact common keys are precisely the old D90 A-support-only submitted population. **95** matched bundles have fully closed Positions on both sides: **98 native Positions each, 55 winners each, identical final target/stop/time counts (50/43/5)**, with H25a reported realized PnL **+13,519.50** versus old **+12,598.66 USDT**, a **+920.85-USDT** difference under the changed shared-account equity/sizing path. Only four H25a closed Positions fall outside this matched-closed cohort and sum to **−1,021.29 USDT**; nine H25a open Positions remain censored. This establishes that the *old matched wins were not magically upgraded by the context flag*; the filter mainly removed old opportunities and freed native account capacity. Across all H25a Positions the native final exits are **50 B targets, 47 stops, 5 market time exits** versus H19a **199/266/31**.

The native **Account locked margin / Account total** fraction, weighted by elapsed time from trade start to period end, averaged only **1.763%** in H25a versus **7.929%** in H19a; median **0.953%** versus **8.845%**, p95 **5.909%** versus **14.531%**, observed maximum **8.480%** versus **16.731%**. H25a spent **51.12%** of elapsed time below **1% native locked margin**, H19a **11.91%**. These numbers reflect *margin reservation* from Nautilus native Account events; they are **not** gross notional exposure, live stop risk, or evidence that unused capital can safely be multiplied into each remaining trade. The annual +13.13% despite higher closed win/payoff/Sharpe is chiefly a **breadth and deployment** problem under this narrow old-plan filter: 655 versus 2,939 submitted and 102 versus 496 closed. Fee reduction is a consequence of fewer native fills, and funding turned **−251.16 versus +46.21 USDT**. The D90 A-only signal is useful research context, but simply deleting other opportunities cannot satisfy the joint Goal on this read; the next child should look for additional *source-grounded* support/range opportunities with native account capacity, while protecting risk bounds and slow winners. No H25a risk increase or ATR/pivot retune is justified by this exposed year.

## Candidate H26a: retain prior-A support plans even when prior-B resistance exists

**Single-round contract registered before H26a code, pilot or Nautilus result:** This is the next unrun child of D90/H25a/D91. The mechanism is that a previously tested A support may remain a valid pullback location even when B had a prior resistance test; the old B high is the planned *target* and possible obstacle, so a prior test need not veto the whole A-to-B retracement. This is a researcher translation of Brooks's previously tested support/range-boundary idea, not a claim that Brooks or Ronnie uses the exact pivot/ATR encoding. The falsifying cases are that added plans fail to become native fills/closures, their additional native closed Positions have nonpositive fee/funding-inclusive net PnL, or the larger shared-account opportunity flow lowers the account return/win profile enough to erase H25a's benefit. D90's already viewed “both” group (415 submitted, 59 old closed, 26 old winners) motivated this child but is **not** H26a's counterfactual result.

**Frozen intervention and legal controls:** Keep H25a's same confirmed eight-left/eight-right pivots, 180 completed four-hour-bar history and prior 14-bar Wilder ATR, but require **at least one prior confirmed A-support pivot within one prior ATR** regardless of prior B-resistance count. Keep original H19a/H25a 50%/61.8% native LIMIT parents, OTO stop and B target, 180-bar validity/hold, 25-bp total stop risk, 5%-of-equity coin notional cap, GTD/cancel behavior, LAST execution, MARK valuation, historical funding and native fee model unchanged. No coin, threshold, tier, exit or sizing search. Compare H26a against **same-code H25a** for this one mechanism, and H19a as the broad reference; rerun controls on the same runner and require exact frozen H25a/H19a parity before economic interpretation. The comparison unit is one Nautilus 100,000-USDT margin account shared by all fixed 37 coins, never a sum of per-coin backtests. Report native bundle/parent-fill/closed/open counts, candidate-only versus common source keys, and censored opens; do not assign old D90 Position outcomes to new orders.

**Main-account outcomes and stopping rules:** Primary score is native final Account equity and annualized net return over the fixed eligible interval; co-gates are native positive closed-Position win rate **55%-65%** (operational meaning of “near 60%”) and annualized net return **strictly above 20%**. Also report fee/funding-inclusive realized payoff, Sharpe365, daily-close maximum drawdown, rejected/denied orders, native account locked-margin occupancy and actual fees/funding. H19a's 15.33% daily-close drawdown is the predeclared risk ceiling for this trial. An integrity, causal-clock, 25-bp/5% bound or open-protection failure stops the economic claim immediately. An unchanged or lower number of closed Positions than H25a refutes the breadth mechanism; fewer than 25 additional closed Positions makes its practical breadth weak. If annual net is no higher than H25a's +13.1282%, or the win gate or risk ceiling fails, stop this child after reporting the failure mechanism. Even a point estimate passing all gates remains exploratory because of prior repeated same-year selection and must await independently collected forward data. No second same-year threshold, coin, tier or exit variant is authorized by this contract.

**Budget, exposed window and data custody:** Candidate budget is **one** BTC/ETH native integrity pilot and, only if it passes, **one** annual 37-coin Nautilus candidate. Control budget is **one** annual H25a and **one** annual H19a same-code replay for exact frozen parity, plus read-only order/clock/economics/paired-week audits. Do not choose annual execution based on pilot PnL. Use the already viewed 37-coin five-minute LAST/MARK/funding Catalogs from 2025-10-07 00:00 UTC through 2026-10-07 08:30 UTC, with order eligibility from 2025-10-17 00:00 UTC; identity receipt SHA-256 `ce9963ca68c66d34af74dbdbfff484f320a622fa73afe64844518aed354ec9fc`. Frozen H25a summary SHA-256 `b5e052a37c7b4448e26cd0a3d07a714429eed9844cf461ccc686bdacfd8dd89a`; H19a summary `658145b32c86bfb947e4b1a909cc05e84a2f9284a6969ac7237ee538407b06fc`; D91 report `632f8fd4fc5c1dc4dacf226a990117762e08830a39b35bacfc8313f67ae9d1f8`. The entire market interval and relevant D90/H25a outcomes are development-exposed. Paired ISO-week uncertainty is descriptive and unadjusted for the many earlier attempts. If the child fails, the next minimal experiment is a separately registered, read-only native path attribution of its added versus lost source opportunities and B-target obstruction, with slow-winner and open-position counterexamples; no automatic parameter search.
